# Active code reduction queue

Resolved proposals are removed from this file after implementation, validation, or a current-tree refutation. The original survey remains available in Git history; this file is intentionally only the active queue.

Inventory after deduplication: 571 active IDs in 525 rows (458 pending, 113 deferred). Resolved and refuted proposals are removed; repeated and superseded rows are merged into their canonical IDs.

Each pending row must be rechecked against the current tree, applied in a compatible batch, validated with the smallest relevant containerized test target, and then removed here.

## Deferred

- **R2482**: **Deferred.** `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol` targets `netstandard2.0`, whose reference surface does not expose `int.TryFormat`/`ISpanFormattable`; a compatible manual invariant formatter would add more code and risk canonical framing regressions. Keep the existing invariant string path until the target framework or a smaller compatible primitive changes.
- **R2525**: **Deferred.** The pinned Roslyn API exposes only `OperationVisitor<TArgument, TResult>` for result-returning visitors; converting the lowerer to a context-free visitor fails with `CS0308` because `OperationVisitor<TResult>` does not exist. Keep the empty context carrier until the dependency exposes a compatible base or a smaller adapter can preserve dispatch.

- **R0001**: R001 | Positional records would change constructor visibility and equality semantics.
- **R0007, R0008, R0009**: R007-R009 | Compiler-probe JSON bytes, artifact authority, and IL opcode admission are compatibility/soundness boundaries; defer to focused format work.

- **R0024**: R024 | `ClosedAbstractDomain.Merge` and `Compare` are public API, and `OwnedCount` supports a load-bearing disposal test.
- **R2433**: R2433 | `WorkerProtocolJson` targets `netstandard2.0`, whose `StreamReader` API has no `ReadToEndAsync(CancellationToken)` overload. - **R1066**: R1066 | Portable IR canonical-image validation intentionally compares deterministic wire bytes. The generated graph and nested rows are mutable and lack equality; a hand-written structural comparer would duplicate every schema field and silently drift. Defer until a schema-generated comparer can preserve nullable fields, nested locations, order-sensitive arrays, documentation IDs, cancellation checks, and mutation coverage.
- **R1090**: R1090 | `CollectSourceMethods` intentionally combines syntax discovery for local functions, property/indexer accessors, primary constructors, and partial-method normalization. A symbol-only traversal would miss or add declared methods; a safe optimization requires a hybrid redesign with explicit result-set/order tests.
- **R0257**: R257 | All linked targets are `netstandard2.0`, where `string.Create`/span APIs are unavailable. A char-buffer implementation would still allocate the buffer and final string and must preserve arbitrary `IEnumerable<byte>` one-shot/enumeration-exception behavior. Defer until a target-compatible allocation contract is designed.
- **R1051**: R1051 | Cache path validation is a TOCTOU/security boundary: cooperative locks do not prevent another process from replacing paths or ancestors, and the repeated checks intentionally bracket capacity staging, eviction, publication, and post-open lock acquisition. Defer until descriptor/FD-based identity operations can replace the checks without weakening fail-closed behavior.
- **R1224**: R1224 | The JSON catalog is currently the only machine-readable authority for the six required evidence IDs; removing the independent ordered-membership assertion would make an empty, reordered, or misspelled list pass. Defer until a separate authoritative schema/catalog exists.
- **R0293**: R293 | Package payload validation now checks source nuspec declarations against packed managed/native files, but `PackageLayoutSmokeTests` still duplicates expected package paths independently from the analyzer props/targets and generated launcher companion inventory. The source-tree and package paths have different roles, and the verifier closure includes native Z3, runtimeconfig/deps files, and other assets, so deriving them mechanically could silently omit or misclassify payloads. Defer until an authoritative package-layout manifest and explicit role/path mapping are designed.

- **R0663**: R663 | **`EffectMethodNodeBuilder.Build` rescans the same method body for three effect dimensions.** The CFG path visits reachable block operations through `AnalyzeControlFlowGraph` to build the ordinary body summary, then `Build` invokes `ScanLexicalControlEffects` over the lexical root and `ScanUsingDisposalEffects` over the full root. Those latter passes independently enumerate operations or disposal structures that the scanner has already visited, with only their effect-specific projections differing. A coordinated body walk or shared per-operation facts can preserve lexical versus reachable semantics, disposal ordering, and direct-witness behavior while avoiding repeated operation-tree work. | `SharpProof.Effects/EffectMethodNodeBuilder.cs:45-114,720-780`; `SharpProof.Effects/OperationEffectScanner.cs:135-220`

- **R1540**: R1540 | **Five of the fifteen analyzer dependency assemblies are older in the two source-tree lanes than in the shipped package, so the self-application lane - whose whole purpose is to prove the product analyzes itself the way a consumer runs it - runs the analyzer on a dependency graph consumers never get.** The three lists R343 names resolve to three directories. `SharpProof.Package/buildTransitive/SharpProof.targets:30-37` points at `$(_SharpProofSharedDirectory)`, which `SharpProof.nuspec:47-56` fills **entirely from `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/bin/$configuration$/netstandard2.0/`**. `eng/self-application/SharpProof.SelfApplication.props:49-56` points at `SharpProof.Analyzer/bin/...`, and `SharpProof.AnalyzerConsumer.props:33-40` at `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core/bin/...`. All three projects target `netstandard2.0` and sit in one solution under central package management, and their lock files disagree: `SharpProof.CompilerCollector` resolves **System.Buffers 4.6.1, System.Memory 4.6.3, System.Numerics.Vectors 4.6.1, System.Runtime.CompilerServices.Unsafe 6.1.2, System.Threading.Tasks.Extensions 4.6.3**, while `SharpProof.Analyzer` and `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core` both resolve **4.5.1, 4.5.5, 4.5.0, 6.0.0, 4.5.4**. The other three System assemblies in the list - `System.Collections.Immutable 9.0.18`, `System.Reflection.Metadata 9.0.0`, `System.Text.Encoding.CodePages 7.0.0` - agree everywhere. **The cause is a transitive floor that reaches one project and not the other.** `SharpProof.CompilerCollector` project-references `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol`, whose direct `System.Text.Json 10.0.10` requires `System.Buffers 4.6.1` and pulls `System.Memory 4.6.3`, `System.IO.Pipelines 10.0.10` and `System.Text.Encodings.Web 10.0.10` with it; `SharpProof.Analyzer` has no such edge and falls back to the `netstandard2.0` defaults. **The mechanism that would fix it is already switched on and inert.** `Directory.Packages.props:6` sets `CentralPackageTransitivePinningEnabled` to `true`, which pins a transitive package only when a `PackageVersion` for it exists - and the file's fourteen entries include none of the five. The three assemblies that agree are the ones either centrally pinned or resolved identically by every path; the five that diverge are exactly the five with no pin. Adding five `PackageVersion` lines makes all 47 lock files agree and makes the divergence impossible to reintroduce. **The shipped notice is correct and this is not a licensing defect** - `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` declares the newer set, and the newer set is what `tools/shared/netstandard2.0/` receives, because every third-party file in the nuspec is sourced from the collector's `bin`. The defect is that the dogfooding and source-tree-consumer lanes silently exercise a different graph, which is precisely the drift R752 predicted - *"if it drifts from the shipping partition, the dogfooding silently stops covering whatever was added"* - measured in versions rather than in property names. | `Directory.Packages.props:5-6,9-23`; `SharpProof.Package/SharpProof.nuspec:33-56`; `SharpProof.Package/buildTransitive/SharpProof.targets:30-37`; `eng/self-application/SharpProof.SelfApplication.props:49-56`; `SharpProof.AnalyzerConsumer.props:33-40`; lock files `SharpProof.Analyzer/`, `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core/`, `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/`, `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/`, `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol/packages.lock.json`; `eng/release/third-party-components.json`; related R343, R752, R491, R1305
- **R1600**: R1600 | **Forty-two of the repository's forty-four `*RegressionTests.cs` files name the behaviour they pin; two name a development wave that is documented nowhere, and both sit beside a correctly named sibling in the same project that already owns their subject.** The convention is otherwise exact - `ArrayAccessCompletion`, `LiftedNullableOperator`, `RefLocalEffect`, `VirtualHierarchyPrecondition`, forty-two in all, each a behaviour. The two exceptions are `SharpProof.Effects.Test/OperationCompletionWaveFiveRegressionTests.cs` and `SharpProof.Worker.Test/CompilerCallableLowererWaveSixRegressionTests.cs`. **Both are grab-bags.** Wave five holds five regressions with no shared subject - a non-exhaustive switch expression with a returning arm, a recursive source call with a base case, a constant null conversion to nullable, a user-defined constant null conversion to a struct, and lifted null division by zero reaching following writes. Wave six holds three: two about signed `Int64` source intervals and one about an expression-bodied `Requires`-only void method. What the members of each file share is the batch they were fixed in, and nothing else. **"Wave" is a concept the repository does not define.** A search across every `.cs`, `.ps1`, `.md` and `.json` finds the word only in these two file names and in `docs/container-development.md:182`, where "parallel wave" means test scheduling - an unrelated use. There is no numbering scheme, no wave one through four, and no document explaining what a wave was. **Each file's subject already has a home next to it.** `SharpProof.Worker.Test/CompilerCallableLowererTests.cs` sits beside the wave-six file, and `SharpProof.Effects.Test/CompositeOperationCompletionRegressionTests.cs` beside the wave-five one. The cost is not the two names: it is that a future regression in either area has no rule telling it whether to join the wave file, join the subject file, or start its own, and a reader looking for the lifted-null-division regression cannot find it by name in a project where forty-two other files can be found exactly that way. Note that the wave-six file's *contents* are already in this ledger three times - R602, R608 and applied R1071 - and none of the three remarks on the name. | `SharpProof.Effects.Test/OperationCompletionWaveFiveRegressionTests.cs:10,40,83,123,163`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/CompilerCallableLowererWaveSixRegressionTests.cs:15,37,89`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/CompilerCallableLowererTests.cs`; `SharpProof.Effects.Test/CompositeOperationCompletionRegressionTests.cs`; the other 42 `*RegressionTests.cs` file names; related R602, R608, R1071
- **R1880**: R1880 | **Thirty-two of the 248 trusted-mutation entries name source text that is absent from the file they target at HEAD, so the preflight those entries feed rejects the catalog on every run of `tooling mutation`.** The check is `Get-MutationTargetIssue` at `Test-SharpProofTrustedMutations.ps1:69-89`: `Content.IndexOf(Needle, StringComparison.Ordinal)`, returning *"target text was not found"* at `-1` and *"target text is not unique"* if a second occurrence follows. `:2221-2257` runs it over **every** entry - the loop is unconditional, it reads each target with `Get-Content -Raw`, and the only earlier gate is `:2211-2216` comparing the catalog size to `mutationEvidence.expectedCatalogCount`, which is 248 and passes. Reproducing that comparison exactly against `git show HEAD:<file>` for the **170** entries whose `Original` is a single-line literal - the remaining 78 use here-strings or escaped double-quoted forms and were not checked - finds 17 absent among those; completing the parse to cover PowerShell here-strings and backtick-escaped double-quoted literals brings the total to **all 248 entries parsed - 170 single-line, 65 double-quoted, 13 here-strings - of which 216 match exactly once, 0 are non-unique, and 32 are absent**. Five were verified independently, by closest-line comparison rather than by the same parser: `nightly-fuzz-command-connected` pins `docker compose run --rm tooling fuzz-nightly` in `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`, which now runs `tooling nightly` because the fuzz campaign moved inside that pipeline command at `Invoke-SharpProofContainer.ps1:139`; `analyzer-rejects-retired-mode` pins `return (options.TryGetValue("sharpproof_mode", out value!) ||` in `AnalyzerConfiguration.cs`, which now looks options up through a `key` parameter; `smt-strict-less-than` pins `_context.MkLt(Integer(left), Integer(right))), defined)` in `IrSmtBackend.cs`, where the operands were renamed to `leftInteger, rightInteger`; `cache-read-lock-coordination` pins the adjacent pair `cacheLock = AcquireLock(_directory);` and `ValidatePath(path);` in `VerificationCache.cs`, where the first line still exists at `:50` and `:186` but no longer immediately precedes the second; and `standalone-build-stage-nonroot-contract` pins `COPY --chown=sharpproof:sharpproof . .` followed by `USER sharpproof` in `eng/container/Dockerfile`, where `USER sharpproof` survives at `:71` without that preceding `COPY`. Each is a refactor that left the catalog behind, and the last two are the ones worth naming twice: one proves a cache lock is taken before a path is validated, the other proves the container build stage drops to a non-root user. **The seventeen single-line cases**: `smt-strict-less-than`, `advisory-contract-candidate-detection`, `contract-api-duplicate-json-rejection`, `advisory-lazy-state-creation`, `effect-replay-allocation-constraint`, `worker-parent-death-boundary`, `release-configuration-empty-expected-set`, `closure-retains-each-component-path-once`, `launcher-checks-launcher-runtime-paths`, `verifier-supervisor-reports-incomplete-cleanup`, `runtime-closure-validates-before-invalidation`, `nightly-fuzz-command-connected`, `contract-constructed-function-pointer-preserves-ref-modifiers`, `analyzer-rejects-retired-mode`, `package-rejects-retired-mode`, `relational-source-call-admission`, `container-archive-source-materialization`. **The fifteen further cases the completed parse adds**: `portable-codec-unused-slot-fails-closed`, `lowering-global-constant-bypass`, `external-contract-closed-precondition-boundary`, `effect-direct-witness-prebody-completion`, `effect-system-object-approved-identity`, `effect-collector-subset-admission`, `contract-api-exported-attribute-parity`, `cache-read-lock-coordination`, `cache-write-lock-coordination`, `launcher-checks-discovered-runtime-paths`, `verifier-supervisor-requires-subreaper`, `relational-summary-instantiation-binding`, `publication-complete-topology-preflight`, `build-task-active-symmetric-topology`, `standalone-build-stage-nonroot-contract`. **Why it was not noticed is R1840's mechanism.** `mutation` runs in exactly two places - the `nightly` pipeline at `Invoke-SharpProofContainer.ps1:136` and the `release-qualification` job at `package-consumers.yml:194`, whose condition at `:124-127` requires a `refs/tags/v` push - so no pull request executes the preflight, and the seventeen refactors that broke these pins each merged green. **What is lost is not the preflight but the mutations.** Each of the seventeen names a soundness property a test is supposed to discriminate - the SMT strict-less-than encoding, the retired analyzer mode rejection, the launcher runtime-path check, the verifier supervisor's incomplete-cleanup report, the container archive materialization branch. Until the pins are refreshed none of those thirty-two mutants can be applied, so the discriminating power the catalog exists to prove is unverified for all of them. | `scripts/Test-SharpProofTrustedMutations.ps1:69-89,2211-2257` and the seventeen entries; `eng/acceptance/contract.json` `mutationEvidence.expectedCatalogCount = 248`; `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofTrustedMutationsParallel.ps1:249-255,316-322` (both invocations reach the same preflight); `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofContainer.ps1:136,478-491`; `.github/workflows/package-consumers.yml:122-127,194`; `.github/workflows/nightly.yml:28`; related R1840, R748, R757
- **R2160**: R2160 | **Fifteen hand-written production types are `public` with no consumer in any other production assembly, in a package that ships no `lib/` folder - so `public` outside `SharpProof.Attributes` buys visibility that nothing can use - and nine of the fifteen can become `internal` today with no build change because the `InternalsVisibleTo` grant they would need already exists.** `SharpProof.Package/SharpProof.nuspec` maps every assembly to `tools\analyzers\dotnet\cs`, `tools\collector`, `tools\shared\netstandard2.0` or `buildTransitive`; there is **no `lib/` target anywhere in either nuspec**, and the only thing a consumer compilation references is the separate `SharpProof.Attributes` package, declared at `:18` as a dependency. Accessibility on everything else is therefore a within-repository decision. **The fifteen**: `EffectRegionSet` (`EffectRegions.cs:63`), `EffectThrowSet` (`EffectValues.cs:102`), `ApiSpecLookupStatus`, `ApiSpecLookupFailureKind` and `ApiSpecResolutionFailureKind` (`ApiSpecResolution.cs:18,23,8`) in `SharpProof.Effects`; `BackendCheckStatus` (`Backend.cs:2`) in `SharpProof.Verify`; `IAbstractDomain` (`IAbstractDomain.cs:7`), `SequenceCardinalityKind` (`SequenceCardinalityValue.cs:2`) and `DataflowAnalysisResult` (`ForwardDataflowAnalysis.cs:16`) in `SharpProof.Dataflow`; `FrontendProgramAbstention` (`FrontendSubset.cs:120`); `IrRelationalSummaryBuildResult` (`IrRelationalSummary.cs:135`); `SpecInstantiationResult` and `SpecInstantiationFailureKind` (`ApiSpecInstantiation.cs:18,8`); and `ContainerContractInfo` (`ContainerContract.cs:6`) and `LinuxWorkerCompletion` (`LinuxWorkerProcess.cs:12`) in `SharpProof.Host`. None appears in any document as an API. **Nine are free.** `ContainerContractInfo`, `LinuxWorkerCompletion` and `DataflowAnalysisResult` have no consumer outside their own project at all; `EffectRegionSet` and `EffectThrowSet` are used only by `SharpProof.Effects.Test`, which `SharpProof.Effects.csproj` already grants `InternalsVisibleTo`; likewise `BackendCheckStatus` for `SharpProof.Smt.Test`, `IAbstractDomain` and `SequenceCardinalityKind` for `SharpProof.Dataflow.Test`, and `FrontendProgramAbstention` for `SharpProof.Frontend.Test`. The remaining six need one grant each - `SharpProof.Effects` to `SharpProof.Specs.Test`, and a first grant from `SharpProof.Summaries` and `SharpProof.Specs`, neither of which has any - in the SDK item form applied R316 standardised. **The asymmetry is the point.** `SharpProof.DeclarativeModels.catalog.json` makes accessibility an authored field and exercises all three values - **62 `public`, 51 `internal`, 17 `private`** - so in the generated layer every `public` is a decision someone wrote down. The hand-written layer has no equivalent, and `public` is what a type gets by not thinking about it. - **R0310**: R310 | `Test-ProductionCSharpComplexity.ps1` compares the measured aggregate against the contract with `-le`: it is a one-sided ceiling, not a fixed ratchet. `eng/acceptance/contract.json` pins `maximumExpressionNodes: 218647`, `maximumDecisionPoints: 12875`, `maximumMembers: 5808`. The rationale's own most recent entry - dated 2026-09-01, this branch - *raised* the ceilings to accommodate added logic, so the branch has already claimed its increases while leaving every decrease unclaimed. The ledger's "Final gate" section asks only for a diff line count and test results; re-measuring and re-tightening the three ceilings to the post-reduction values belongs there too. A heading containing a markdown link therefore produces different slugs from the two implementations. This matters because both are gates over the same artifacts: `Generate-Readme.ps1` validates that every in-document anchor link resolves, while `DiagnosticDescriptorCatalogAssertions` - compiled into three test projects - computes anchors for diagnostic help links. This is an **unstated second reason** why the `dependabot.yml` pin matters: that file blocks `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.*` at `>= 4.15.0` and justifies it only as "Analyzer binaries must remain loadable by the documented Roslyn 4.14 host", with no mention that a test also binds a Roslyn internal by reflection. | `SharpProof.Analyzer.Test/FinalCompilationCollectorTests.cs:905`; `SharpProof.Gates.Test/PerformanceGateTests.cs:463,972,988`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/ProtocolModelSchemaTests.cs:199,233,275`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/CompilerArtifactModelSchemaTests.cs:203,262`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/ContainerContractTests.cs:18`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/WorkerProgramTests.cs:389`; `SharpProof.Specs.Test/ApiSpecTests.cs:1006`; `SharpProof.Testing.Test/IrCSharpDifferentialOracleTests.cs:72`; `SharpProof.Frontend.Test/FrontendLoweringTests.cs:1267`; `SharpProof.Analyzer.Test/RuntimeFlagshipOracleTests.cs:107`; `SharpProof.Analyzer.Test/RuntimeRequiresOracleTests.cs:67`; `.github/dependabot.yml`

- **R0748**: R748 | **Both ratchets are one-sided, refresh is manual and milestone-driven, and neither reports the headroom that would prompt a refresh - which is measurable right now.** The repository has two independent complexity ratchets and they share the shape. The repo-wide one compares `-le` against three ceilings in `contract.json`. The per-file one, `ArchitectureTests:2072-2110` over `eng/acceptance/algorithm-size-ratchets.json`, tests `if (fileExpressionNodes > entry.MaximumFileExpressionNodes)` across **16 files with 4 caps each**, and its only well-formedness assertion on those 64 numbers is `Is.Positive` (`:552-555`). Neither mechanism emits measured-versus-cap, so slack accumulates invisibly between hand-driven refreshes. **The current slack is demonstrable without running either gate.** The size-ratchet manifest was last touched by `8a4c836db` on 2026-09-01. Since that commit, **9 of its 16 files have changed, for a net of +117/-330 = -213 lines**, through commits whose titles are this ledger's own application work - `Remove unused abstract-domain operations`, `Simplify interval domain normalization`, `Share Roslyn reference lowering fallback`, `Share frontend unsupported value classification`, `Apply audited code reductions`. `IntervalDomain.cs` alone is +4/-79 and `SequenceCardinalityDomain.cs` is +0/-72; a 79-line deletion cannot leave a file's expression-node count unchanged, though the exact figure needs the Roslyn count the gate performs rather than the line delta measured here. Meanwhile the repo-wide ceilings moved **upward** twice after that date and were not refreshed. So the loop closes on itself: **applying this ledger is what creates the unreclaimed headroom, and nothing surfaces it.** The useful change is not a lower bound - that would fight the deliberate-raise workflow the rationale field documents - but reporting: have both gates emit measured values beside their caps, so a refresh is prompted by data instead of by remembering. `Test-ProductionCSharpComplexity.ps1` already builds a `-Json` result object carrying the measured counts, so the repo-wide half is nearly free. | `scripts/Test-ProductionCSharpComplexity.ps1:178-181,182-206`; `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest/ArchitectureTests.cs:508-560,2072-2110`; `eng/acceptance/algorithm-size-ratchets.json`; `eng/acceptance/contract.json` `productionComplexity` and 13 `productionCoordinatorComplexity.layers` entries; commits `8a4c836db`, `7293f09e0`, `e7aae6430`, `5c7189e9c`, `a070fc530`

- **R0950**: R950 | **One disabled-by-default analyzer rule accounts for seven accommodations across six files, and two of them changed the shape of production code.** `Directory.Build.props:20` sets `AnalysisLevel=latest-all` with the comment *"Run every built-in .NET analyzer rule, including rules disabled by default."* CA1508 (*Avoid dead conditional code*) is one of those rules, and it appears in **no** configuration file - not `.editorconfig`, not `.globalconfig`, not `Directory.Build.props` - so it is live solely by that policy. What it costs, measured: **five `SuppressMessage` attributes** across four files, whose justifications describe what should be one false positive in **three different wordings** - *"misreads the multi-branch nullable assignment above the null check as unreachable"* appears **verbatim three times** (`RequiresCallSiteDiscovery.cs:1569-1573`, `OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs:454-458` and again at `:526-530`), alongside *"does not track the nullable expression assignment across the two declaration forms"* and *"does not track the nullable expression selected from the declaration syntax"*. **And two structural accommodations**, which are the substantive half: `ContractCanonicalization.cs:17-19` keeps recursive specialization *"in an object instead of captured local functions"* because *"CA1508 otherwise builds a pathological dataflow graph for this code during every qualifying compilation"*, and `ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs:1195-1197` keeps a large control-flow dispatcher *"out of the captured traversal closure"* because *"CA1508 otherwise constructs an expensive interprocedural flow graph for the local function during every qualifying build"*. Two production classes in two assemblies are shaped by one analyzer's cost model rather than by their domain, and the reason survives only in a comment. That is accidental complexity in the strict sense. Scoping CA1508 - `dotnet_diagnostic.CA1508.severity = none` in `.globalconfig`, or per-file in `.editorconfig` beside the eleven suppressions already there - would remove five attributes and free two files to be shaped by what they do. The trade is real and should be stated when it is made: CA1508 is off by default upstream because of exactly this false-positive rate, but turning it off forfeits whatever true positives it finds in a codebase whose entire subject is dead-branch analysis. | `Directory.Build.props:18-20`; `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core/RequiresCallSiteDiscovery.cs:1569-1573`; `SharpProof.Effects/OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs:454-458,526-530`; `SharpProof.Effects/ConditionalTruthOperatorFacts.cs:39-43`; `SharpProof.Effects/ManagedAbstractFlow.cs:2188-2192`; `SharpProof.Contracts/ContractCanonicalization.cs:17-19`; `SharpProof.Effects/ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs:1195-1197`
- **R0951**: R951 | **The repository is split almost exactly in half between two opposite conventions for declaring project references, and nothing records which is intended.** Of 39 projects holding at least one compile-visible `ProjectReference`, **19 declare only what is not already reachable transitively** and **20 additionally declare references that another of their own direct references already provides**. The concentration is uneven in a way that looks like drift rather than policy: `SharpProof.Worker.Test` has 8 of 13 redundant, `SharpProof.CompilerCollector` 6 of 9, `SharpProof.Worker` 4 of 8, `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core` 3 of 5, while nineteen projects have none. **The naive form of this measurement is wrong and the correction matters**: a first pass that treated every `ProjectReference` as flowing reported 68 redundancies. The figure of 42 excludes them. **Neither convention is wrong, and this is not a proposal to delete 42 lines.** Explicit references document real usage and survive an intermediate project dropping a dependency; minimal ones keep the graph small and honest. The repository has in fact already taken the explicit position in one place - `BoundaryEnforcementTests.ThinAnalyzerHasOnlyCurrentFrontendDependencies:226-232` pins `SharpProof.Analyzer`'s **direct** reference set by exact equality, which only means something if direct references are deliberate. The finding is that this is pinned for exactly one project out of 39, and the other 38 are evenly divided with no stated rule, so a reviewer cannot tell whether an added redundant reference is intentional documentation or an oversight. | 60 `.csproj` files; 118 direct compile references, 42 transitively redundant; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/SharpProof.Worker.Test.csproj`; `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/SharpProof.CompilerCollector.csproj`; `SharpProof.Worker/SharpProof.Worker.csproj`; `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core/SharpProof.Analyzer.Core.csproj`; `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest/BoundaryEnforcementTests.cs:226-260`

- **R0956**: R956 | **The verifier's process exit-code contract - 124 for timeout, 125 for environment failure - is written as bare integer literals at roughly forty sites across C#, bash, and PowerShell, is named nowhere, and is documented nowhere.** `124` appears in production at `SharpProof.BuildTasks/RunVerifier.cs:325`, `VerifierProcessSupervisor.cs:149`, `SharpProof.Gates/Performance/WorkerPerformanceProbe.cs:164,268`, and `SharpProof.Host/LinuxWorkerProcess.cs:165`, and is asserted at eight further sites in `SharpProof.Package.Test/BuildTaskTests.cs` and `LauncherArgumentTests.cs`. `125` appears at `RunVerifier.cs:306,1025,1046` and at **ten sites in `VerifierProcessSupervisor.cs`** (`:27,36,54,83,107,138,142,187,204`), plus **two `exit 125` in `eng/container/entrypoint.sh` and twelve in `eng/container/loop-command.sh`**. - **R0968**: R968 | **`MaximumTermDepth = 64` enforces three different notions of depth at three places in one file, raising three different exception types, and two of them share one message.** `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/CompilerArtifact/CompilerSpecificationPackProvider.cs:13` declares the constant once and uses it three ways. At `:362-367` it is passed as `JsonDocumentOptions.MaxDepth`, so exceeding it raises **`JsonException`** from `JsonDocument.Parse` - a type this file never throws itself. At `:563-566`, `ParseTerm(JsonElement, int depth)` guards its own recursion and throws **`InvalidDataException("A specification-pack term is too deep.")`**. At `:266-268`, `Instantiate(Term, ImmutableArray<IrVarId>, int depth)` guards *the same recursion shape over the already-parsed tree* and throws **`ArgumentException`** with the **identical message string**. The two traversals are structurally parallel - `ParseTerm` recurses `depth + 1` through unary, binary and conditional operands at `:619-641`, and `Instantiate` does the same at `:281-289` - so they count the same nesting, and every `Term` reaching `Instantiate` in this file came from `ParseTerm`. **The ordering makes the later guards hard to reach**: JSON nesting depth for a term is strictly greater than term-tree depth, so the reader's `MaxDepth` fires first, then `ParseTerm`'s, leaving `Instantiate`'s as a defensive check on data already twice rejected. That is defensible as belt-and-braces; what is not is that the three tiers report differently. A user who exceeds the limit gets `JsonException` with a framework message, or `InvalidDataException`, or `ArgumentException` - the last two with the *same* sentence, so the message cannot distinguish which limit was hit, while the type distinguishes them in a way no caller acts on: this repository catches `ArgumentException` at 35 sites and `InvalidDataException` at 3. Across all 538 production exception messages this is **the only text used with more than one exception type**. | `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/CompilerArtifact/CompilerSpecificationPackProvider.cs:13,266-268,281-289,362-367,563-566,619-641`

- **R0977**: R977 | **"Does this object have exactly these properties?" is implemented six times under three verbs, and the six do not agree on ordering, case, or failure mode.** The implementations are `Assert-ExactJsonObjectProperties` (`Assert-SharpProofFuzzRunnerResult.ps1:3`), `Assert-ExactJsonProperties` (`Assert-SharpProofStandaloneGateResult.ps1:3`), `Assert-ExactProperties` (`Generate-ApiSpecCatalog.ps1:159`), `Assert-ExactMembers` (`Generate-DiagnosticDescriptors.ps1:20`), `Require-ExactProperties` (`Complete-SharpProofPilotReview.ps1:23`), and `Test-SharpProofExactProperties` (`SharpProof.PublicationPlanIdentity.psm1:27`). **Three axes diverge.** *Ordering*: `Assert-ExactMembers` and `Require-ExactProperties` `Sort-Object` both sides before joining on `\|`, so property order is irrelevant; `Test-SharpProofExactProperties` joins **unsorted** and compares with `-ceq`, so it rejects a correct object whose properties are in a different order. *Case*: `Assert-ExactMembers` compares with `-ne` (case-insensitive) while `Require-ExactProperties` - otherwise line-for-line the same sort-join-compare - uses `-cne` (case-sensitive), so a property differing only in case passes one and fails the other. Repository-wide the balance is **12 `[Parallelizable]` against 41 `[NonParallelizable]`**, so the suite predominantly opts out - which makes the four assemblies that declare a worker count the exception rather than the rule, and makes the 4-versus-8 disagreement invisible to anyone reading either file alone. | `SharpProof.Analyzer.Test/AssemblyInfo.cs:3-4`; `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest/AssemblyInfo.cs:3`; `SharpProof.Package.Test/AssemblyInfo.cs:3`; `SharpProof.Worker.Test/AssemblyInfo.cs:3`; `eng/test/architecture-parallel.runsettings:4`; `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofSemanticTests.ps1:40`; `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest/BuildSchedulingTests.cs:903` **VERIFIED, and one half is gated while the other is not.** `eng/test/architecture-parallel.runsettings` sets `NumberOfTestWorkers` to **8**; `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest/AssemblyInfo.cs:3` declares `[assembly: LevelOfParallelism(4)]`. Four test assemblies carry `LevelOfParallelism(4)`: `Analyzer.Test`, `ArchitectureTest`, `Package.Test` and `Worker.Test`.
- **R0979**: R979 | **`CreationFlowCaptures` repeats the same capture-ID ambiguity state machine already duplicated by `CoalesceAssignmentFlowCaptures` and `ConditionalTruthOperatorFlowCaptures`.** All three classes own a `HashSet<CaptureId>` for ambiguous IDs and a dictionary keyed by `CaptureId`; they retain the first eligible definition, reject or mark a later conflicting definition, and refuse to resolve an ambiguous capture. The **24** commands named in any maintained document and the **9** pinned by `scripts/Test-SharpProofReadme.ps1` - `acceptance`, `build`, `check`, `contract`, `coverage`, `mutation`, `pack`, `samples`, `test` - are the third and fourth lists. Every pinned command is documented, which is the safe direction; the unsafe one is that **15 commands are documented and unpinned**, so a document naming `sp quick`, `sp pr`, `sp nightly`, `sp security`, `sp pilots`, `sp performance` or `sp test-changed` can drift from the dispatcher with nothing failing. Counting every `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest` assertion that mentions a command as a quoted literal raises coverage to 29 of 36, but that coverage is incidental - each test pins whatever command it happened to need - and **seven commands are pinned by nothing at all**: `check`, `corpus`, `corpus-update`, `gates`, `pilot-review`, `portable-tests`, `self-apply`. **Three of those seven are worse than unpinned, because the dispatcher passes them through as data.** `:427` computes `$gateMode = if ($Command -ceq 'gates') { 'all' } else { $Command }` and hands the result to `SharpProof.Gates` as its argv[0], so `corpus`, `corpus-update` and `performance-smoke` are simultaneously CLI command names and gate-mode names; renaming either side silently changes what runs. `SharpProof.Gates/Program.cs:83-84` declares its own usage as `[all|corpus|corpus-print|corpus-update|performance|performance-smoke]` - **six** modes, of which **`corpus-print` is reachable from nothing in the repository**: no container command, no script, no workflow, no test names it outside `Program.cs:59` and that usage string. The repository already knows how to check a vocabulary against its authority - `DevCheckCommandPlanTests` executes `Get-SharpProofDevCheckPlan.ps1` and asserts its JSON, and `SemanticArchitectureShardsCoverEveryFixture` asserts a roster against reflection - so asserting that `build.ps1`'s set is a subset of the container's, that the documented set is a subset of the dispatched set, and that the pass-through names match `Program.cs`'s modes is three assertions of a shape already in use. | `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofContainer.ps1:4,426-434`; `build.ps1:4-9`; `scripts/Test-SharpProofReadme.ps1`; `SharpProof.Gates/Program.cs:30-33,47,59,66,73,83-84,98`; `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest/ArchitectureTests.cs`, `BuildSchedulingTests.cs` for the incidental pins; related R321, R577

- **R1780**: R1780 | **Seventy-seven analyzer tests pass, as the set of diagnostics to enable, exactly the set of diagnostics they then assert - and the host suppresses everything else - so none of the seventy-seven can fail because the analyzer started reporting something new.** `AnalyzerTestHost.CreateCompilation:62-92` takes an `enabledIds` collection and, when it is non-empty, calls `WithSpecificDiagnosticOptions` mapping **every** id in `new SharpProofAnalyzer().SupportedDiagnostics` to `ReportDiagnostic.Warn` if it is in the set and **`ReportDiagnostic.Suppress` if it is not**. `AnalyzerTestHost.AssertIds:19-26` then asserts `diagnostics.Select(d => d.Id)` `Is.EqualTo(expected)` - an exact, ordered sequence comparison, which reads as a strict oracle. - **R1840**: R1840 | **Twenty-five of the 248 trusted-mutation entries pin exact source text inside ten generated files, so a generator whose output formatting shifts breaks a preflight in a lane that pull requests never run.** `scripts/Test-SharpProofTrustedMutations.ps1` declares 248 entries over 108 distinct target files, each carrying an `Original` string that must occur verbatim in its `File`. `:2240-2257` runs `Get-MutationTargetIssue -Content -Needle ([string]$mutation.Original)` over every entry before any mutation is applied and throws *"Trusted mutation target preflight failed"* listing each bad one - a real and well-built check. **Ten of the 108 targets are generated files**, carrying 25 entries between them: `ContractApiMetadata.generated.cs` (5), `CSharpScalarSemantics.generated.cs` (4), `PortableIrModel.generated.cs` (4), `CompilerWireMappings.generated.cs` (3), `LauncherArguments.generated.cs` (2), `IrOperatorCatalog.generated.cs` (2), `EffectContractMappings.generated.cs` (2), and one each in `ProtocolModel.generated.cs`, `OperationSupportCatalog.generated.cs` and `EffectEvaluationProjections.generated.cs`. - **R2800**: R2800 | **`eng/acceptance/algorithm-size-ratchets.json` states that the effect scanner was split so that "both layers retain independent reviewable ceilings", and the class has three partials of which only two carry one - the uncovered partial being three times the size of the covered one.** `SharpProof.Effects/OperationEffectScanner` is a single `partial class` across `OperationEffectScanner.cs` (1,683 lines, ratcheted), `OperationEffectScanner.Expressions.cs` (896 lines, **no entry**) and `OperationEffectScanner.Assignments.cs` (292 lines, ratcheted at 682 file expression nodes / 32 decision points). The file's own `rationale` names the intent: "Assignment evaluation is separated from the core effect scanner so both layers retain independent reviewable ceilings." Expression evaluation is the third layer of that split and is unbounded. **The same gap appears at project scale.** Across the five semantic-core projects - Effects, Dataflow, Ir, Verify, Frontend - there are 16 ratchet entries, and the largest files are mostly uncovered: `ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs` (3,373 lines), `ManagedAbstractFlow.cs` (3,028), `OperationCompletionEvaluator.cs` (1,538) and `EffectMethodNodeBuilder.cs` (1,149) all have no entry, while the largest *ratcheted* file in the repository is `OperationEffectScanner.cs` at 1,683. `ManagedAbstractFlow.cs` owns `MaxAnalyzedBlocks = 256` and `MaxAnalyzedOperations = 4096`, the CFG budget `docs/analysis-limits.md` documents, so it is squarely the "algorithm file" the rationale describes. **Caveat on method:** line count is a proxy - the ratchet measures Roslyn expression nodes and decision points, which a read-only survey cannot compute, so the correct caps are unknown and the size ordering may not match the node ordering. The partial-class half does not depend on the proxy: it is a stated design intent with one of three declared layers uncovered. `eng/acceptance/contract.json` `productionComplexity` bounds the repository total but sets no per-file limit, so nothing else constrains these files individually. **Related:** R0748 covers the same ratchets from the other side (one-sided, no headroom reporting), R0310 the aggregate ceiling, R0757 the three ratchet mechanisms; this row is about files with no cap at all. | `eng/acceptance/algorithm-size-ratchets.json` (16 entries and its `rationale`); the three `SharpProof.Effects/OperationEffectScanner*.cs` partials with two entries between them; `SharpProof.Effects/ManagedAbstractFlow.cs:15-16`; `eng/acceptance/contract.json`; related R0748, R0310, R0757

Comparing the netstandard2.0 `resolved` versions in `SharpProof.Analyzer/packages.lock.json`, `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core/packages.lock.json` and `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/packages.lock.json` - the last being the project whose `bin` `SharpProof.Package/SharpProof.nuspec` copies the entire shared payload from - gives **exactly five**: `System.Buffers` 4.5.1 against 4.6.1, `System.Memory` 4.5.5 against 4.6.3, `System.Numerics.Vectors` 4.5.0 against 4.6.1, `System.Runtime.CompilerServices.Unsafe` 6.0.0 against 6.1.2, and `System.Threading.Tasks.Extensions` 4.5.4 against 4.6.3. Both source-tree lanes agree with each other and both are behind what ships. **The wider measurement.** Across all 47 `packages.lock.json` files, six packages resolve to more than one version, and the split is **not** explained by target framework: seven (package, tfm) pairs conflict *within* one framework. On netstandard2.0 the five above split into an older group (`Analyzer`, `Analyzer.Core`, `ContractForGenerator`, `CompilerProbe.TestAsset`) and a newer group (`CompilerArtifact`, `CompilerCollector`, `Worker.Protocol`), with `System.Numerics.Vectors` carrying a **third** group at 4.4.0 (`Dataflow`, `Ir`, `Smt`, `Specs`). `System.Reflection.Metadata` splits 8.0.0 against 9.0.0 on **both** net8.0 and net9.0 - `SharpProof.Smt.Test` is the lone net9.0 project still on 8.0.0. **Why it matters beyond tidiness.** These assemblies are loaded side by side in one Roslyn process: the analyzer entry assemblies ship to `tools/analyzers/dotnet/cs` and their dependency closure to `tools/shared/netstandard2.0`, and the payload for both comes from the collector's build. So the versions a consumer runs are the newer group, while self-application and the consumer-props lane run the older one - which is precisely the divergence R1540 says defeats the purpose of self-application. Central Package Management pins the 14 direct references but nothing unifies these transitives; `Directory.Packages.props` has no `PackageVersion` entry for any of the six. | the 47 `packages.lock.json` files; `SharpProof.Analyzer/packages.lock.json`, `SharpProof.Analyzer.Core/packages.lock.json` and `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/packages.lock.json` for the five-package comparison; `SharpProof.Package/SharpProof.nuspec` for the payload source; `Directory.Packages.props` (14 entries, none of the six); related R1540, R0343, R0491

- **R2980**: R2980 | **Contract calls are `[Conditional("SHARPPROOF_CONTRACTS")]`, so a test compilation that parses contract source without defining that symbol silently drops the calls - and the knowledge that it must be defined lives in one helper line, with 29 of the 36 `CSharpParseOptions` construction sites not setting it and nothing checking the pairing.** `SharpProof.Attributes/Contract.cs:9` declares `public const string ConditionalSymbol = "SHARPPROOF_CONTRACTS"` - shipped public API per `SharpProof.Attributes/PublicAPI.Shipped.txt:2` - and `SEMANTICS.md:173`, `docs/public-api.md:25` and `docs/coverage-and-limits.md:172` all record that the contract entry points carry `Conditional("SHARPPROOF_CONTRACTS")`. `eng/testing/TestCompilation.cs:96-98` is the one place that supplies it, as `new CSharpParseOptions(languageVersion, preprocessorSymbols: [Contract.ConditionalSymbol])`. Across the repository there are **36** `new CSharpParseOptions` sites; **7** pass `preprocessorSymbols` and **29** do not. **Stated carefully: this row does not claim any current test is vacuous.** Establishing that would require tracing, for each site, whether the source it parses actually contains `Contract.Requires`/`Ensures`/`Assume`, which this survey has not done - 13 of the 29 sit in files that contain such fixtures somewhere, but file-level co-occurrence is not proof that the parsed text does. The reduction-relevant fact is structural: a requirement whose omission is **silent** - the compiler removes the calls and the test still passes - is encoded in a single helper line and enforced by nothing. A test that parses contract source without the symbol asserts against code the compiler deleted. There are no `[Conditional]`-guarded **call sites** in that text, only the declarations, so `preprocessorSymbols` is irrelevant there and omitting it is correct. The structural point stands - the requirement is silent when omitted and enforced nowhere - but any remedy should target sites that parse **consumer** code calling `Contract.Requires`/`Ensures`/`Assume`, not every parse-option construction, and the per-site trace to identify those has not been done.
- **R3000**: R3000 | **Production null-argument checking uses three mechanisms - 261 `ArgumentNullGuard` calls, 40 `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull` and 33 `throw new ArgumentNullException` - and 39 of the 73 alternative sites are in projects that *can* reach the guard, while 34 are in projects that structurally cannot.** The split is the useful part, because it separates a choice from a constraint. **Can reach `ArgumentNullGuard` and uses something else anyway (39 sites):** `SharpProof.Worker` 20 alternatives against 7 guard calls, `SharpProof.Summaries` 8 against 4, `SharpProof.Testing` 4 with **zero** guard calls, `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher` 4 with zero, plus two stragglers in `SharpProof.Ir` (which *hosts* the guard, 66 calls) and one in `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact` (20 calls). Worker and Summaries are the notable ones - both reference `SharpProof.Ir`, both already call the guard, and both use the alternative more often than the guard in Summaries' case. **Structurally cannot reach it (34 sites, correctly excluded):** `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol` 16, `SharpProof.Host` 7, `SharpProof.BuildTasks` 5, `SharpProof.Attributes` 2, `SharpProof.Gates` 2, `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers` 1, `SharpProof.CompilerProbe.TestAsset` 1. `ArgumentNullGuard.cs` lives in `SharpProof.Ir` and is `Compile Include`-linked into only `SharpProof.Dataflow` (`SharpProof.Dataflow.csproj:11-12`, under `SHARPPROOF_DATAFLOW_ARGUMENT_GUARD`), so every other consumer reaches it through a project reference to `SharpProof.Ir` - and these seven projects have none. **That is itself worth recording:** the repository's own null-guard is unreachable from its lowest layers, so roughly a third of production null checking cannot use it by construction, and `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol` alone accounts for 16 hand-written throws. - **R3040**: R3040 | **"Does this type derive from that one?" is implemented twice, and the two answers disagree on generic types.** `eng/RoslynSymbolFacts.cs:7-32` provides `IsOrDerivesFrom(ITypeSymbol? type, ITypeSymbol? possibleBase, bool includeSelf = true)`, which walks `BaseType` comparing **`current.OriginalDefinition`** against **`possibleBase.OriginalDefinition`**; it is `Compile Include`-linked into `SharpProof.Frontend` and `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers` and called from `SharpProof.Frontend/ContractApiIdentityResolver.cs` and `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers/CancellationBoundaryAnalyzer.cs`. `SharpProof.Effects/EffectValues.cs:317-328` provides `EffectTypeFacts.IsDerivedFrom(INamedTypeSymbol type, INamedTypeSymbol expectedBase)`, the same `BaseType` walk, comparing the **constructed** symbols with no `OriginalDefinition`; it has five call sites inside `SharpProof.Effects`. **The divergence is real, not cosmetic.** Comparing original definitions makes `List<int>` match `List<T>`; comparing constructed symbols does not. The shared version is also the more defensive one - it tolerates null on both sides and offers `includeSelf`, neither of which the Effects copy has. **The fix is one line of csproj.** `SharpProof.Effects.csproj:12` already `Compile Include`-links `../eng/RoslynCfgFactory.cs`, so linking `../eng/RoslynSymbolFacts.cs` beside it is the established mechanism; the five call sites then move to the shared predicate and `EffectTypeFacts.IsDerivedFrom` is deleted. - **R3120**: R3120 | **R3000's three-mechanism null-guard split, re-measured: it is enforced by an analyzer on one side of the target-framework line and completely ungoverned on the other, and 22 of the 33 remaining hand-written sites are in the one syntactic form the enforced mechanism cannot express - which is the exact form the repository's own helper was built for.** `Directory.Build.props:18-21` sets `EnableNETAnalyzers`, `AnalysisLevel=latest-all` (commented "Run every built-in .NET analyzer rule, including rules disabled by default") and `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild`, and `:28` and `:165-166` turn warnings into errors for every test and production project. **CA1510** ("use the `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull` throw helper") is therefore a build error - but only where the helper exists, which is `net8.0`/`net9.0`. On `netstandard2.0` `ThrowIfNull` does not exist, CA1510 cannot fire, and **nothing replaces it**: no analyzer, no architecture test, no `.editorconfig` entry and no `BannedSymbols.txt` line requires `ArgumentNullGuard.NotNull` over a hand-written throw. So the mechanism the repository *owns* is the one mechanism it does not enforce, and 32 of the 33 non-`Tools` hand-written sites sit on that unenforced side. **The form split is the sharper half.** Classifying all 33: **11 are statement form** and **22 are the `?? throw` / `? throw` expression form** (`SharpProof.Worker.Protocol` 14 of 16, `SharpProof.Summaries` 3 of 8, `SharpProof.Attributes` 2 of 2, `SharpProof.Testing` 2 of 2, `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact` 1 of 2). `ThrowIfNull` returns `void` and cannot be used in an initializer, which is why CA1510 does not flag that form at all - and `ArgumentNullGuard.NotNull` *returns the value*, so it is strictly the better tool at precisely those 22 sites. **The single clearest illustration is one file:** `SharpProof.Testing/IrCSharpDifferentialOracle.cs:29` writes `_factory = factory ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(factory));` while `:36-37` call `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull` seven lines later - two mechanisms in one type, chosen by nothing but whether the check sits in a field initializer, in a project that has a direct `ProjectReference` to `SharpProof.Ir` (`SharpProof.Testing.csproj:11`) and an `InternalsVisibleTo` grant (`SharpProof.Ir.csproj:27`), so `NotNull` was available for both. **Three corrections to R3000's census, one of which shrinks the finding.** (1) It excluded `Tools/`, where `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz` holds **30** further hand-written throws - nearly doubling the repository total to 63 and making it the largest single cluster. Those 30 are **correctly excluded**: `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz/SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj:11` suppresses `CA1510;CA1512` explicitly and `:8-10` documents why ("deliberately uses ... explicit argument guards with stable messages ... It has no public API contract"). This is the only opt-out in the repository and it is a disclosed decision, not drift - worth recording so the 30 are not re-derived as a defect. (2) R3000 places `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol` (16 sites) and `SharpProof.Meta.Analyzers` (1) in the "structurally cannot reach" column. That holds for the project-reference path, but both already use `Compile Include ... Link` against files outside their own directory - `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol.csproj:11-14` links **`HashEncoding.cs` and `Utf16WellFormedness.cs` out of `SharpProof.Ir` itself**, and `Meta.Analyzers` links three `eng/` files - so a third link line reaches `ArgumentNullGuard.cs` by a pattern already proven inside those same two `ItemGroup`s, and the guard file already carries the per-consumer `#if` arm that such a link needs. That moves 17 sites from constraint to choice, leaving 17 genuinely blocked rather than 34. (3) `SharpProof.Gates` is listed at 2 alternatives; it is **5** (`Corpus/CorpusCatalog.cs:28`, `Corpus/OpenSourceCorpusCatalog.cs:51,66`, `Performance/PackageBuildEstimator.cs:89,177`) - and all five are `ThrowIfNull` statement form on `net9.0`, which is exactly what CA1510 mandates, so Gates should leave the defect entirely rather than be recounted. | `Directory.Build.props:18-23,28,165-166`; `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz/SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj:8-11`; `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol/SharpProof.Worker.Protocol.csproj:11-14`; `SharpProof.Testing/IrCSharpDifferentialOracle.cs:29,36-37`; `SharpProof.Testing/SharpProof.Testing.csproj:11`; `SharpProof.Ir/ArgumentNullGuard.cs:231-248` (`NotNull` returns `T`); `SharpProof.Ir/SharpProof.Ir.csproj:20,27` (IVT for Summaries and Testing); the 11/22 statement-versus-expression classification across the seven projects; corrects and extends R3000

- **R3200**: R3200 | **The ownership-guarded delete R0727 describes is only half a convention: the shared file supplies the delete and not the matching create, so every one of its 14 call sites writes the owning root name twice by hand - in three different disciplines - and at two sites the second copy is derived from the path being checked, which makes the guard a tautology.** `eng/testing/TestRepository.cs:59-90` takes `(path, rootName, errorMessage)` and rebuilds `Path.GetFullPath(Path.Combine(Path.GetTempPath(), rootName))`, then refuses to delete anything whose relative path escapes it. So `expectedRoot` recomputes to `_expectedParent` itself, the relative path is always the GUID segment, and **the containment check cannot fail**. The guard's whole value is that the caller states independently which root it believes it owns; recovering that name from the path under test removes the independence and leaves a check that reads like an assertion and asserts nothing. **A second helper covers the other half and the two do not meet.** `eng/testing/TempDirectory.cs:1-26` is an `IDisposable` that creates *and* deletes, is used 102 times, and has **no** containment guard - its `TempDirectory(prefix, parentDirectory)` overload will create under any parent - while `Directory.Build.props:97-102` links it into only four test projects (`SharpProof.Package.Test`, `SharpProof.ArchitectureTest`, `SharpProof.Gates.Test`, `SharpProof.Worker.Test`) against `TestRepository.cs`'s ten. - **R3320**: R3320 | **`IrBinaryOperator` is enumerated four times; three are generated and one of those is machine-checked exhaustive against `Enum.GetValues`. The fourth is hand-written, covers 13 of 14, and the member it omits is exactly the one the rest of the system singles out.** The enum has **14** members (`SharpProof.Ir/IrOperatorCatalog.generated.cs:25-39`). Three enumerations are complete and derived: `IrOperatorCatalog.Get(IrBinaryOperator)` (`:84-107`, 14 arms, generated from `SharpProof.Frontend/CSharpScalarSemantics.json`), `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/PortableIrModel.generated.cs:176-191` (14-element ordered wire array, generated from `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerArtifactModel.schema.json:3935-3953`), and that schema block itself. **The wire array is not merely generated - it is proved exhaustive at runtime.** `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/PortableIrGraphCodec.cs:33-36` defines `IsComplete<T>(T[] values) where T : struct, Enum => values.SequenceEqual(Enum.GetValues(typeof(T)).Cast<T>())`, `:18-24` applies it to four wire catalogs (`OpaquePurities`, `UnaryOperators`, `BinaryOperators`, `HavocKinds`), `:26-31` applies a sibling `IsCompleteSlots` to three more (`IrTermKind`, `IrLocationKind`, `IrInstructionKind`), and four assertions across `SharpProof.Worker.Test/CompilerArtifactModelSchemaTests.cs:145,171` and `SharpProof.Worker.Test/PortableIrGraphCodecTests.cs:294,302` enforce both. **Seven enum catalogs in this subsystem are checked for exhaustiveness; the eighth enumeration of the same enum is not.** `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/CompilerArtifact/CompilerSpecificationPackProvider.cs:670-689` (`ParseBinaryOperator`) is a hand-written string-to-enum table of **13** identity cases - every arm is literally `"X" => IrBinaryOperator.X` - omitting **`StringConcat`**, with a `_` arm that throws `InvalidDataException("A specification-pack binary operator is unsupported.")`. Its sibling `ParseUnaryOperator` (`:659-668`) is complete at 2 of 2, so the gap is specific rather than a general shortcut. **The omitted member is the distinguished one.** `SharpProof.Specs/ApiSpecTermValidator.cs:217` computes a spec term's allocation flag as `binary.Operator == IrBinaryOperator.StringConcat` - string concatenation is the single allocating binary operator in the IR vocabulary, which is why it exists as a member at all. So the runtime ingestion path for compiler-artifact specification packs cannot express the one operator the specification model treats specially. **R0393 already records that this provider duplicates the relational spec-pack AST, JSON parser and term instantiator from `SharpProof.Specs`; this is a concrete instance of the two copies having diverged**, and it diverged in the direction that narrows the vocabulary. **Severity is low today and the row should say so:** no checked-in pack uses it - `SharpProof.Specs/RelationalSpecPackCatalog.json` and `SharpProof.Specs/DefaultApiSpecCatalog.json` between them use only `GreaterThanOrEqual` and `Equal` - and the behaviour is fail-closed, so the effect is a rejected pack rather than a wrong answer. The point is that 13-of-14 is invisible: nothing distinguishes a deliberate exclusion from a missed case, and the repository already owns the check that would (`IsComplete<T>`), applied seven times a few files away. - **R3340**: R3340 | **Analyzer suppression uses four mechanisms; 10 of the 30 suppressed rules use more than one of them, one uses all four, and in two projects the split runs between sibling files with the same justification.** A census over `.editorconfig`, every `<NoWarn>` in a `csproj`/`props`/`targets`, every `#pragma warning disable`, and every `[SuppressMessage]` finds **30** distinct rules suppressed somewhere and **10** suppressed through more than one mechanism: CA1031, CA1308, CA1515, CA1720, CA1822, CA1849, CA2000, CA5394, RS1035, RS2008. **CA1849 uses all four** - `.editorconfig:69-70` for `SharpProof.Worker{,.Launcher}/Program.cs`, `<NoWarn>` in `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz/SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj:11`, two `#pragma` pairs in `SharpProof.Gates/Performance/WorkerPerformanceProbe.cs:256-264,424-431`, and a `[SuppressMessage]` at `SharpProof.Gates/Program.cs:23`. **The sharpest illustration is one project and one rule.** `.editorconfig:42-44` disables CA1515 for four files by path - `SharpProof.BuildTasks/{InvalidatePublishedResult,ResetPublishedVerification,RunVerifier,ValidatePublishedVerificationResult}.cs` - under the comment "MSBuild discovers these task entry types by public name." The fifth file in that project, `SharpProof.BuildTasks/CancelableBuildTask.cs:7-10`, is the **abstract base class those four derive from**, and it suppresses the same CA1515 with a `[SuppressMessage]` whose justification says the same thing - "Public MSBuild tasks require a public base type." Same rule, same project, same reason, two mechanisms, and the file that explains why the other four are public is the one that differs. Adding a fifth task means guessing which convention applies. The same shape recurs: **CA1308** is `.editorconfig:76-78` for three `SharpProof.Worker/*.cs` files and `[SuppressMessage]` in `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/OpenSourceCorpusCatalog.cs:355` and `OpenSourceCorpusImporter.cs:82`; **CA1822** is `.editorconfig:46-48` for one `SharpProof.Dataflow` file and four attributes across `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz`; **CA2000** is `.editorconfig:62-66` for `SharpProof.Smt/IrSmtBackend.cs` and `#pragma` pairs in `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerManifestArtifact.cs:89-129`. A fifth scope compounds it: `Directory.Build.targets:19-22` blanket-`NoWarn`s CA1515, CA2007 and CA5394 for every `IsTestProject`. **What is consistent, and worth preserving:** every one of the eleven `.editorconfig` blocks carries an explanatory comment and every `[SuppressMessage]` carries a `Justification`, so no suppression is undocumented - the reasons are good, only the choice of mechanism is arbitrary. **Why the arbitrariness costs:** the four differ in properties that matter. `.editorconfig` scopes by path glob and is invisible from the suppressed file; `[SuppressMessage]` is local, self-documenting and moves with the code; `#pragma` is line-ranged and the only one that can scope below a type; `<NoWarn>` is project-wide and cannot be narrowed. So answering "why is this rule off here?" means checking four places, and a rule moved between files can silently lose or gain a suppression. All 15 file paths named in `.editorconfig` do exist, so nothing is stale - this is convention drift, not rot. The reduction is a stated rule: local and self-documenting (`[SuppressMessage]`) for a type or member, `.editorconfig` only where a whole file genuinely needs it, `#pragma` only for sub-member scope, `<NoWarn>` only for a whole project - then move the 10 multi-mechanism rules onto it. | `.editorconfig:42-78` (11 blocks, 15 paths, 10 rules); `Directory.Build.targets:19-22`; `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz/SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj:11`; `SharpProof.BuildTasks/CancelableBuildTask.cs:7-10`; `SharpProof.Gates/Program.cs:23`; `SharpProof.Gates/Performance/WorkerPerformanceProbe.cs:256-264,424-431`; `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/OpenSourceCorpusCatalog.cs:355`; `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/OpenSourceCorpusImporter.cs:82`; `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerManifestArtifact.cs:89-129`; the 30-rule / 4-mechanism census; related R0950, R3120, R3220

The five rules that appear in both `.globalconfig` and `<WarningsAsErrors>` - `CA1811`, `CS8019`, `IDE0051`, `IDE0052`, `IDE0060` - are the documented two-step the file explains at `.globalconfig:7-10` ("the build may still promote it to an error through WarningsAsErrors"): the config turns the rule on at warning level, the property promotes it. - **R3540**: R3540 | **Six exception messages stating IR invariants are written twice each, and one of the six is a whole validity check - condition, exception type, message and parameter name - duplicated across an assembly boundary.** A census of every handwritten production C# file finds **21** string literals of at least 25 characters and four words that appear in more than one file. Eleven are analyzer-suppression rule ids or `Justification` text, where repetition is inherent to `[SuppressMessage]` and not a defect. One is `"Trusted platform assemblies are unavailable."` across five files, already owned by R0341 and R0729. **Six are IR invariant messages, and they are this row.** **The sharpest is a duplicated check, not just a duplicated string.** `SharpProof.Ir/IrProgramInterpreter.cs:24-27` guards `if (!ReferenceEquals(program.Factory, _factory)) throw new ArgumentException("The program belongs to a different IR factory.", nameof(program));` and `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/PortableIrGraphCodec.cs:65-68` guards `if (program != null && !ReferenceEquals(factory, program.Factory)) throw new ArgumentException("The program belongs to a different IR factory.", nameof(program));` - the same invariant, the same exception type, the same message and the same parameter name, in two assemblies. The invariant is soundness-relevant: an `IrProgram` used with the wrong factory carries interned identifiers from another scope, so this is a check worth owning in exactly one place, on `IrProgram` or `IrFactory`. **Two more cross the same boundary as exhaustiveness fallbacks.** `"Unknown IR location kind."` is thrown from the `default:` arm at `SharpProof.Ir/IrProgramBuilder.cs:296-298` and produced by the fallback callback at `PortableIrGraphCodec.cs:490`; `"Unknown IR instruction kind."` likewise at `IrProgramBuilder.cs:253-255` and `PortableIrGraphCodec.cs:518`. So the codec restates three invariants that `SharpProof.Ir` owns, across an `InternalsVisibleTo` grant that would let it call the owner - the same enum-exhaustiveness family as R3320. **The remaining three are inside `SharpProof.Ir` and are defence in depth, which the row should say plainly.** `"Sequence access requires a sequence value."`, `"Sequence access requires an integer index."` and `"Length requires a string or sequence value."` each appear in `IrFactory.cs` (`:634`, `:635`, `:607`) and `IrInterpreter.cs` (`:497`, `:502`, `:465`). These are **not** redundant checks: the factory validates a term's static *type* at construction and throws `ArgumentException`, while the interpreter validates a runtime *value kind* and returns `InvalidValue(...)`. Different layer, different input, different failure mode - only the wording is shared, and sharing it means two behaviours are described by one sentence that can drift on one side. **The codebase already treats two of these six as data:** `IrFactory.cs:630-636` passes the two sequence-access messages as *arguments* to `IrTermServices.ValidateSequenceTerms(this, sequence, index, ..., ...)`, so a shared constant has an obvious home in `IrTermServices` and half the plumbing exists. **One further pair worth a line, outside the IR:** `"Could not locate the SharpProof repository root."` appears in `SharpProof.Gates/RepositoryLayout.cs` and `eng/testing/TestRepository.cs:28-29` - a production repository-root finder and the shared test one throwing the same sentence, which is the R0725 shape from the opposite direction. | `SharpProof.Ir/IrProgramInterpreter.cs:22-27`; `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/PortableIrGraphCodec.cs:63-68,490,518`; `SharpProof.Ir/IrProgramBuilder.cs:253-255,296-298`; `SharpProof.Ir/IrFactory.cs:601-607,628-636`; `SharpProof.Ir/IrInterpreter.cs:459-465,495-502`; `SharpProof.Gates/RepositoryLayout.cs`; `eng/testing/TestRepository.cs:28-29`; the 21-literal production census; related R0341, R0729, R0871, R3320, R0725

All 41 inline users currently sit one directory below the root, so nothing is broken today - but a script moved one level deeper silently resolves to the wrong root and proceeds, where its C# counterpart would throw. The two languages therefore disagree about what makes a directory *the* repository root, and the side with no check has 62 of the 64 sites. **A second, smaller instance of the same shape in the same layer:** the guard `if ($Fast -and $NoBuild) { throw '-Fast and -NoBuild cannot be combined.' }` is written identically in four entry scripts - `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofChangedTests.ps1:25-27`, `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofContainer.ps1:54-56`, `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofPackageTests.ps1:33-35` and `scripts/Invoke-SharpProofSemanticTests.ps1:31-33` - and three of the four sit directly beneath an identical `Import-Module ... SharpProof.ContainerExecution.psm1` plus `Assert-SharpProofContainer` preamble. - **R3600**: R3600 | **Three independent complexity authorities govern production C#; none of them says anything about the 55 percent of the C# that is test code, which holds thirteen of the twenty largest files and the only two files over 4,000 lines - and unlike every other exemption in this repository, that one is undeclared.** **The controls, all production-scoped.** (1) `eng/acceptance/contract.json`'s `productionComplexity` caps the aggregate at **218,647** expression nodes, **12,875** decision points and **6,026** members across "handwritten production C#", enforced by `scripts/Test-ProductionCSharpComplexity.ps1` from `eng/acceptance/Verify.ps1:652`. (2) `eng/acceptance/algorithm-size-ratchets.json` sets four per-file and per-member ceilings on **16** named production files, from `SharpProof.Frontend/RoslynOperationLowerer.cs` to `SharpProof.Verify/ProofKernel.cs`. (3) `contract.json`'s `productionCoordinatorComplexity` sets two more on **13** named coordination layers (R0312). No test `.cs` file appears in any of the three. **The measurement.** Handwritten C# splits **274 test files / 119,844 lines** against **348 production files / 96,918 lines** - tests are **55.3 percent** of the C# in the repository. Of the twenty largest `.cs` files, **thirteen are tests**. The three largest files in the entire repository are tests: `SharpProof.Effects.Test/EffectAnalysisTests.cs` at **8,933** lines, `SharpProof.Worker.Test/WorkerTests.cs` at **7,139**, and `SharpProof.Package.Test/WorkerMsBuildIntegrationTests.cs` at **4,388**. The largest production file, `SharpProof.Effects/ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs`, is **3,361** - so the largest test file is **2.7 times** it, and it is bounded by nothing while `ExceptionHandlerReachability.cs` is itself already noted by R0312 as one of six large production files outside the coordinator ratchet. **30** test files exceed 1,000 lines against **21** production files. **The rationale is what makes this worth recording.** `algorithm-size-ratchets.json` states its own purpose: "These formatting-invariant complexity ratchets keep algorithm files and members reviewable." Reviewability is not a production-only property, and the least reviewable artifact in the repository - an 8,933-line file - is outside all three controls. **Two honest counterpoints, because the fix is not the obvious one.** First, line count is a weaker proxy for complexity in tests than in algorithms: test files are legitimately long and flat, and all three controls measure expression nodes and decision points rather than lines, precisely because lines are a poor measure. Second, **decomposing these files has already been declined**: R0088, R0091, R0095, R0096 and R0105 are deferred with "These parameterize or abstract large test fixtures; keep named failure isolation and local arrange/assert evidence in this reduction pass." So the reduction here is **not** "split the big test files". It is that three authorities are silent about the majority of the codebase and **nothing says they are deliberately silent**. Every other exemption in this repository is stated: `SharpProof.NonProductionDefaults.props:8-9` declares the samples/pilots opt-out that R0750 analyses; `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/README.md:46-47` calls its weak floors "starting floors" openly; `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz/SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj:8-10` documents its analyzer suppressions. A `rationale` line in `algorithm-size-ratchets.json`, or a `testComplexity` block that measures without capping, would turn an unstated scope into a decision - and would make it visible if the 55 percent ever starts growing in ways the 45 percent is not allowed to. | `eng/acceptance/contract.json` (`productionComplexity`, `productionCoordinatorComplexity`); `eng/acceptance/algorithm-size-ratchets.json` (16 files, `rationale`); `scripts/Test-ProductionCSharpComplexity.ps1`; `eng/acceptance/Verify.ps1:652`; the 274/119,844 versus 348/96,918 split, the 20-largest-file ranking and the 30-versus-21 count; `SharpProof.NonProductionDefaults.props:8-9`; `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/README.md:46-47`; `Tools/SharpProof.Fuzz/SharpProof.Fuzz.csproj:8-10`; related R0312, R0973, and the deferred R0088/R0091/R0095/R0096/R0105

- **R3720**: R3720 | **The identity of every API specification is derived from the spelling of C# type names that a PowerShell generator emits as string literals - through reflection and a string replace - in the single file the acceptance contract designates as the API-specification identity authority.** `SharpProof.Specs/ApiSpecContentDigest.cs:97` computes each term's hash tag as `term.GetType().Name.Replace("Declaration", "Term")`. That value goes straight into a `CanonicalHashWriter` and out through `ApiSpecTable.cs:28` as `ContentSha256`, which `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerManifestArtifact.cs:21,42` folds into the compiler manifest hash as `apiSpecContentSha256` and the worker surfaces as `Summary.Versions.ApiSpecContentSha256`. **The file is not incidental**: `eng/acceptance/contract.json` gives the trusted-computing-base component `apiSpecificationIdentity` exactly **one** path, and it is this file. **The names it reflects on are generator output.** The nine declaration records - `SpecVariableDeclaration` through `SpecLengthDeclaration` - are emitted into `SharpProof.Specs/DefaultApiSpecCatalog.generated.cs:126-160` by `scripts/Generate-ApiSpecCatalog.ps1`, where the type names exist as hard-coded fragments in PowerShell string literals at `:383-458` (`"new SpecVariableDeclaration($role, $ordinal, $type)"`, `"new SpecBooleanDeclaration($literal)"`, and so on). So renaming a record in a PowerShell template, or changing the `Declaration` suffix convention, changes every API-specification digest and therefore every compiler manifest hash. **Nothing would notice.** The only test of this digest, `SharpProof.Specs.Test/ApiSpecContentDigestTests.cs:11-35`, asserts relative properties only - that reordering and duplicating exception metadata names yields the same digest and that different content yields a different one - and it varies only `exceptionMetadataNames`, never reaching the term-tag path. No absolute digest value is pinned anywhere in the repository, so a rename produces a silently different identity that every existing assertion still satisfies. **The repository already states the principle this violates.** `BannedSymbols.txt:46-47` bans `ISymbol.ToDisplayString` under the heading "Display text is presentation only and must not become a semantic identity", and the meta-analyzer ships `SPMETA004`, "Semantic string controls behavior" - and `SharpProof.Specs` is one of the 17 projects that set `SharpProofUsesMetaAnalyzer`, so that analyzer runs on this file. A census sharpens it: **`GetType().Name` appears seven times in handwritten production code, and six are message text** - exception names in `AnalyzerConfiguration.cs:124`, `FinalCompilationCollector.cs:57`, `Worker.Launcher/Program.cs:106`, and three fuzz/oracle messages. This is the only one that becomes an identity. **The fix needs no new machinery.** Three lines below the reflective tag, `:99-114` already switches exhaustively over all nine declaration types to extract their payloads and children. An explicit tag string per arm - the same closed vocabulary the IR uses for its own kinds - lives inside a `switch` the method already writes, and makes the digest depend on a declared constant rather than on what a generator happened to call a class. | `SharpProof.Specs/ApiSpecContentDigest.cs:93-124` (tag at `:97`, switch at `:99-114`); `SharpProof.Specs/ApiSpecTable.cs:28`; `SharpProof.Specs/DefaultApiSpecCatalog.generated.cs:126-160`; `scripts/Generate-ApiSpecCatalog.ps1:383-458`; `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerManifestArtifact.cs:21,42`; `SharpProof.Specs.Test/ApiSpecContentDigestTests.cs:11-35`; `eng/acceptance/contract.json` (`apiSpecificationIdentity`, one path); `BannedSymbols.txt:46-47`; the seven-use `GetType().Name` production census; related R3540, R3320

All four sit over **generated** enums (`SharpProof.Effects/EffectContractMappings.generated.cs:72-79,108-127`; `SharpProof.Specs/DefaultApiSpecCatalog.generated.cs:15-31`), and **all four are correct today** - I checked: `EffectContractKind` has 16 non-zero members, `EffectAnalysisIncompleteReason` has 4, and `DefinedEffects` omits nothing from `SpecEffect`'s twelve. **The finding is which of them is machine-checked.** `SharpProof.Effects.Test/EffectContractWireParityTests.cs:101-114`, `DecoderMasksCoverExactlyTheNeutralVocabulary`, asserts `AllEffects == Enum.GetValues<EffectContractKind>().Aggregate(None, (all, v) => all | v)` and the same for `AllCapabilities`. That is exactly the right check - the mask is proved equal to the enum it claims to cover. **`AllIncompleteReasons`, three lines below the two it guards in the same file, is not in that test**, and neither is `DefinedEffects`. **The uncovered one is the one generated code depends on.** `EffectContractMappings.generated.cs:268` emits `new(typeof(EffectAnalysisIncompleteReason), (long)EffectContractMetadata.AllIncompleteReasons, true, [])` - so the generator writes a row referencing a hand-written constant whose value must match an enum the same generator writes. Add a fifth incomplete reason and the generator emits both the new member and a row still advertising a four-bit vocabulary; nothing compares them. `SharpProof.Effects/EffectSummary.cs:92` consumes it too. **The two uncovered masks fail in opposite directions, which is worth stating because it changes how each would be noticed.** `(1 << 4) - 1` fails **open**: a fifth member leaves the mask at 15, so a constant meaning "all" quietly stops meaning all. `DefinedEffects`' explicit OR fails **closed**: a thirteenth `SpecEffect` is simply absent from the mask, so `ApiSpecTable.cs:297` throws "The effect facet contains undefined flags" for a flag that is in fact defined - safe, but the message points away from the cause. **The remedy is already written in this repository.** Extending `DecoderMasksCoverExactlyTheNeutralVocabulary` with a third `Enum.GetValues<T>().Aggregate` assertion covers `AllIncompleteReasons` in three lines, and the same three lines in a `SharpProof.Specs.Test` fixture cover `DefinedEffects`. The style split is worth resolving at the same time: two masks in two assemblies express one idea as bit arithmetic with a magic width and as an explicit member list, and only the arithmetic form can be silently wrong. | `SharpProof.Effects/EffectContractValues.cs:14-19`; `SharpProof.Specs/ApiSpecTable.cs:5-17,297`; `SharpProof.Effects.Test/EffectContractWireParityTests.cs:101-114`; `SharpProof.Effects/EffectContractMappings.generated.cs:72-79,108-127,268`; `SharpProof.Effects/EffectSummary.cs:92`; `SharpProof.Specs/DefaultApiSpecCatalog.generated.cs:15-31`; `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/CompilerArtifact/CompilerWireMappings.generated.cs:167,207`; related R3320, R3720

Link` mechanism - rather than a one-line swap. **A second reason not to reach for the obvious fix:** `IsCompilerInAttribute` is called from `RemoveCompilerInAttribute`, which is called from `ParameterRefCustomModifiersMatch(IParameterSymbol, IParameterSymbol)` - no `Compilation` is in scope anywhere on that path, so replacing the string walk with `Compilation.GetTypeByMetadataName` plus `SymbolEqualityComparer` means threading a compilation through three frames. Centralizing the *name* is the cheap half; symbol identity is a separate decision. **What makes this an outlier rather than a convention:** it is the only production site that identifies one specific BCL type by walking namespaces.
