Three allowlists then carve generated types back out: `:664` returns early from the whole field rule when `field.ContainingType?.Name == "OperationSupportCatalogData"`, and `:669` and `:703` skip `StringFieldInIr` when the containing type's name `is not ("IrUnsupportedInfo" or "IrExceptionInfo")`. **All three are generated types, and their names live in data files.** `OperationSupportCatalogData` is emitted into `SharpProof.Frontend/OperationSupportCatalog.generated.cs:10` by `scripts/Generate-OperationSupportCatalog.ps1` from `SharpProof.Frontend/OperationSupport.catalog.json`; `IrUnsupportedInfo` and `IrExceptionInfo` are emitted into `SharpProof.Ir/IrModel.generated.cs:122,135` by `scripts/Generate-IrModel.ps1` from `SharpProof.Ir/IrModel.schema.json`, where they appear literally as `"name": "IrUnsupportedInfo"` at `:93` and `"name": "IrExceptionInfo"` at `:115`. So the chain is **JSON schema to PowerShell generator to generated C# to a bare `.Name` comparison in an analyzer in a different assembly**, and nothing along it declares the dependency. Rename a type in the schema and the exemption evaporates; the rule then fires on generated code, reporting a diagnostic against a file whose own header says "Do not edit this file directly". **The exemption is also invisible where it applies.** Nothing in the three generated files - or in the schema and catalog that produce them - indicates that these type names are load-bearing for a soundness rule. That encodes a real justification - a weak cache keyed by `Compilation` is compilation-scoped rather than global mutable state - and it is why the repository's sixteen `ConditionalWeakTable` caches pass `SPMETA002` legitimately. **Four other name comparisons in the file are not part of this finding**, and the row says so: `:201` `"GetSemanticModel"`, `:318` `"Equals"`, and `:1073,1076` `"request"`/`"cancellationToken"` match *API shape* on symbols already resolved through `KnownSymbols`, which is how a signature must be identified. Only the three allowlists key behaviour to a name with no structural check. **The irony is load-bearing, not rhetorical**: this analyzer ships `SPMETA004`, "Semantic string controls behavior", whose description is that closed enums and typed records must represent semantic reasons - and its own exemptions are three string literals. Those tests can remain green if the product stops adding cleanup time or moves overflow detection after startup. Product behavior is in `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher/Program.cs:252-263`, which computes the envelope/final limit before `LinuxWorkerProcess.Start`. Replace these language-level repetitions with assertions against the product timeout boundary and an observable no-start condition, preserving overflow and cleanup-grace coverage; do not merely delete the promised coverage. This is redundant test code plus a coverage gap, not evidence that the current launcher calculation is wrong. Suggested validation: containerized launcher/package tests with ordinary and overflow budgets. Static evidence verified at HEAD 16b8e787c; no test execution.
- **R3860**: R3860 | **"Decode UTF-8 text that may carry a byte-order mark" is implemented four times in production under three mutually incompatible policies, and the two implementations that read the *same file* disagree about whether it is valid.** The compiler manifest is written by `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/FinalCompilationCollector.cs:42` through `AtomicFile.WriteUtf8` (`SharpProof.Ir/AtomicFile.cs:4,70-76`), whose `Utf8 = new(false)` never emits a preamble. It is then read by two consumers that both call `CompilerManifestArtifactFile.ReadAllBytes` (`SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerManifestArtifact.cs:1014-1027`, which returns raw bytes and does no preamble handling) and both hand the result to `CompilerManifestArtifactJson.Deserialize` - **but decode it differently**. `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher/Program.cs:1108` calls `new UTF8Encoding(false, true).GetString(bytes)` with no preamble handling, so a BOM survives as a leading U+FEFF and the JSON parse throws; `SharpProof.Worker/WorkerInputSnapshot.cs:7,42,57-61` strips exactly one BOM first (`Utf8Preamble => [0xEF, 0xBB, 0xBF]`, `bytes.AsSpan().StartsWith(...) ? 3 : 0`) and accepts it. Two more policies exist elsewhere: `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/CorpusSnapshotFormat.cs:31,51` **rejects** a preamble through a hand-written byte triple (`bytes[0] == 0xEF && bytes[1] == 0xBB && bytes[2] == 0xBF`), and `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol/ProtocolJson.cs:40,65` strips an **unbounded** number of leading marks at character level (`.TrimStart('\uFEFF')`). Reject, skip-one, strip-many, and don't-handle - for one concept, with no shared helper. **Checked negative: the divergence does not reach a digest.** `WorkerInputSnapshot.cs:31,51` hashes `manifestBytes` - the raw bytes *including* any preamble - through `WorkerProtocolJson.ComputeSha256` and `CompilerArtifactInputHash.Compute`, and `Program.cs:1109` likewise hashes `bytes`, so cache identity covers the preamble whichever decoder ran; this is a consistency finding, not a soundness one. **The consequence is that the worker's tolerance is dead.** On the launcher-driven path the launcher reads and rejects a BOM-prefixed manifest at `:1108` before the worker re-reads the same path, and no in-repo producer emits a preamble at all - so `Utf8Preamble` and its offset arithmetic have no reachable effect today beyond making two readers of one artifact disagree about what a valid manifest is. **Reachability splits the 12 production `UTF8Encoding` constructions into a choice and a constraint.** `SharpProof.Worker` and `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher` both project-reference `SharpProof.Ir` and `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol`, and `SharpProof.Gates` reaches both transitively through them, so those **8** sites could share one decoder - a choice. `SharpProof.Host` declares **zero** `ProjectReference` (`SharpProof.Host/SharpProof.Host.csproj`), so its 4 sites (`LinuxPathIdentity.cs:484,555,613,656`) genuinely cannot reach a shared helper; **this vindicates R0887's file-local scope as a constraint rather than an oversight, and R3860 does not restate it.** Two further symptoms of the missing helper: **five named statics for one encoder under four names** - `AtomicFile.cs:4` and `CorpusFileTransaction.cs:13` both `Utf8`, `PerformanceGate.cs:67-68` and `WorkerPerformanceProbe.cs:530-531` both `Utf8WithoutBom`, `ProtocolJson.cs:12` `s_strictUtf8` - where the two spelled `Utf8` are the only **lenient** ones (`new(false)`, substituting U+FFFD) and their names give no hint that they do not throw, even though `AtomicFile.WriteUtf8`/`WriteUtf8Async` are the canonical writers for manifests, responses and the verification cache; and **`WorkerInputSnapshot.cs:7` plus `CorpusSnapshotFormat.cs:31` both hand-roll the preamble that `Encoding.UTF8.Preamble` already exposes**, both assemblies targeting net9.0 where that property exists. A shared helper hosted in `SharpProof.Ir` or `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol` (both netstandard2.0) would need `GetPreamble()` rather than the span property. | `SharpProof.Worker/WorkerInputSnapshot.cs:7,31,42,51,57-61`; `SharpProof.Worker.Launcher/Program.cs:1108-1109`; `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/CorpusSnapshotFormat.cs:31,51`; `SharpProof.Worker.Protocol/ProtocolJson.cs:12,40,65`; `SharpProof.Ir/AtomicFile.cs:4,70-76,84-88`; `SharpProof.Gates/Corpus/CorpusFileTransaction.cs:13`; `SharpProof.Gates/Performance/PerformanceGate.cs:67-68`; `SharpProof.Gates/Performance/WorkerPerformanceProbe.cs:530-531`; `SharpProof.CompilerArtifact/CompilerManifestArtifact.cs:1014-1027`; `SharpProof.CompilerCollector/FinalCompilationCollector.cs:42`; `SharpProof.Host/SharpProof.Host.csproj`

## Continued adversarial review - 2026-09-07

Ten disjoint read-only reviewers checked current code and build infrastructure; the coordinator alone appends this section. Review started at 4548d281685687dffb2e25c382a887cd32d3249a and observed the external implementation commit 2730a4e94b3a94a2fac0d74b2ee632b79b2c3616 before this append. Preserve concurrent implementation changes and recheck cited sites before acting. Findings below are static evidence, not executed regressions or measured performance results.

- **R4075**: R4075 | **R0557 overstates publication-plan work and proposes weaker freshness checks.** Three package identities produce six loop validations, not twelve. Artifact validation checks all seven paths and lengths; it does not hash package contents. The manifest is separately parsed. Checking only the current artifact could allow an earlier push before detecting deletion/resizing of a later artifact, which the present whole-bundle checks reject first. Stage permissions do not make directory entries immutable to their owner. Separate stable schema/decision validation from live checks only while retaining whole-bundle freshness, manifest checks, and existing push/failure boundaries. The changed-symbol fixture appends bytes and does not establish same-length tamper detection. | `scripts/SharpProof.PackageIdentity.psm1:10-14; scripts/Publish-SharpProofRelease.ps1:574,825-842; scripts/SharpProof.PublicationPlanIdentity.psm1:198-227,367; scripts/Test-SharpProofPublicationPlanIdentityFixtures.ps1:187`; qualifies R0557, separate from R4011.
- **R4076**: R4076 | **R0841's array-pass fusion changes malformed-input rejection order.** Assert-SharpProofJsonArray validates every element kind before any element's property validation. With an invalid-property object followed by a scalar, current code rejects the scalar first; a fused pass rejects the earlier object's properties first and can change error wording. Retain the initial kind pass; if worthwhile, extract a property-validation core for already admitted objects while preserving the guarded helper for other callers. Apply the same constraint to payloads and nested entries. Expected-rejection fixtures accepting any exception cannot prove equivalent failure ordering. | `scripts/SharpProof.ReleaseJson.ps1:43,73-105,149-175`; qualifies R0841, related R0977.
- **R4077**: R4077 | **Lane renewal conflates rejecting an alias with owning its cleanup.** On a factory result already owned by this or another lane, Renew disposes the result before returning BackendUnavailable. Another lane can still be verifying against that backend outside the renewal lock. Rejecting an alias should not acquire or dispose its ownership; its existing owner remains responsible. Correct this ownership boundary before R0800 shares lane-state construction. Preserve renewal classification, timeout evidence, and retirement ordering. The existing reuse test uses one lane and a non-disposable backend, so it does not discriminate disposal of another active lane's backend. This static counterexample requires an invalid factory return; normal-factory failure was not demonstrated. | `SharpProof.Worker/SharpProofWorker.cs:346,644-680; SharpProof.Worker.Test/WorkerTests.cs:5491,6339`; qualifies R0800.
- **R4079**: R4079 | **R0281's direct AtomicFile.WriteUtf8 substitution changes the probe writer's guarantees.** The probe captures an absolute destination and uses exclusive CreateNew/FileShare.None staging plus writer flush and explicit disk flush. WriteUtf8 uses File.WriteAllText and publishes with the original path after staging resolved a full path; it has neither the same exclusive creation nor explicit disk flush. An intervening current-directory change also distinguishes relative-path publication. Share publication/cleanup primitives while retaining the probe's absolute destination and flushed exclusive write. The existing staged byte writer is a possible seam only after encoding, failure order, staging names, and source-link dependencies are checked. Neither path establishes full directory-rename crash durability. | `SharpProof.CompilerProbe.TestAsset/CompilerProbeAnalyzer.cs:70-109; SharpProof.Ir/AtomicFile.cs:26-80`; qualifies R0281/R0879.
- **R4080**: R4080 | **The compilation-wide companion cache captures cancellation owned by one analyzer session.** CompanionTypes stores an ExecutionAndPublication Lazy whose factory closes over the creating policy's token. Session A can construct it, return early for a direct/closed precondition without evaluating it, and then cancel. Session B with the same Compilation and a live token can evaluate that Lazy and receive A's cancellation; the Lazy retains the exception. Publish only completed immutable companion inventories across sessions and keep cancellation with the requesting session. The type-closure cache's no-partial-result rule must also exclude cached cancellation failures. Preserve source-companion mode, comparers, and direct-precondition short-circuit order. With overlapping operations on the same corpus directory, recovery after the final move but before marker deletion can restore old outputs and delete the marker; the writer can then delete the already absent marker and report success. Recovery earlier in publication can instead remove stages/backups still needed by the writer. Introduce a shared writer/recovery ownership protocol and coordinate readers that need a consistent snapshot; marker existence alone is not proof of abandonment. Preserve crash recovery, retryable failed-rollback backups, unique staging and existing path admission. This condition requires same-directory overlap; isolated tooling workspaces avoid it. Distinct from R2436 recovered-path admission, R4192 staging inventory and R4199 required deletion. Proposed coordinated writer/recovery tests at mid-publication and final-move/pre-marker-delete boundaries, plus abandoned-marker controls, are unexecuted; static concurrency candidate only.

- **Corpus durability boundary, not a demonstrated additional bug.** CorpusFileTransaction.cs uses WriteThrough/file flushing, but its marker move, destination moves, marker deletion and backup cleanup have no explicit directory synchronization. The [Linux fsync documentation](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/fsync.2.html), checked 2026-09-07, distinguishes file flushing from persistence of containing-directory entries. Accordingly, ordinary process-interruption recovery must not be presented as evidence of power-loss ordering. CorpusGateTests.cs:214 constructs an already-present interrupted batch and calls Recover; it does not simulate storage-loss ordering. If system-crash durability is intended, specify and validate marker/backup/destination directory ordering; otherwise document the narrower recovery boundary. No storage fault, machine interruption or filesystem-specific outcome was tested. R4269 remains the distinct live-writer ownership issue.
